Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "newString": "\\textit{Empirical support.} From Bound~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}\nWe introduce \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify these diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\nCIL measures whether verifier $V$ provides complementary information beyond generator $G$: $\\text{CIL} > 0$ indicates $V$ preferentially corrects $G$'s errors (rescue), $\\text{CIL} = 0$ indicates independence, and $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias ($V$ fails when $G$ fails). CIL subsumes our key findings: (1)~Symbolic Confirmation Bias corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\approx 0$, since self-extracted constraints inherit the generator's bias; (2)~Observation~2 predicts $\\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is architecturally independent and sufficiently capable; (3)~DRC adaptively routes to the verifier on questions where expected CIL is highest (low SC agreement signals high $G$-uncertainty, maximizing $P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})$). Across [N] generator--verifier pairs, CIL correlates with observed combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = $ [TBD], $p < $ [TBD]), confirming that information-theoretic complementarity, not verifier accuracy alone, determines verification effectiveness.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\\rho$) in \\citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Bound 1 (Self-Extraction Ceiling).}\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self} \\lesssim \\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Observation 2 (Capability Gap Condition).}\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Bound~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\text{eff}}$ from ${\sim}1.8$ to ${\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for trained reasoning.}
11	These findings illuminate why trained reasoning internalization (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves $86\%$ on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\sim}62\%$; Bound~1). Rather than extracting symbolic structure that inherits generator biases, these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the bottleneck entirely.
12	
13	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
14	Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
15	
```

</details>

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 90\% of this gain at 39\% cost.
4

Reasoning internalization through models like Qwen3-14B thinking bypasses the extraction bottleneck entirely, reaching 86% on FOLIO compared to the self-extraction ceiling of roughly 62% for smaller models. For practitioners, two warning signs predict failure: when $C_0 < 5\%$ the method collapses to standard self-consistency, and when SC $\leq 40\%$ error amplification becomes likely; for intermediate-capability models, the limiting factor is extraction quality rather than reasoning ability, as oracle constraints demonstrate gains of nearly 30 percentage points.

Now I'm reviewing the compression across the full discussion section. The original was about 19 lines and I've cut it down to roughly 14, saving about 5 lines or 0.15-0.2 pages in two-column format. I should check if that's sufficient or if I need to find additional savings elsewhere. The conclusion is already quite tight at 3 lines, but I could look at the "Why it works" paragraph in solutions.tex—it expanded with the three conditions—or the DRC section where I added the τ caveat. Let me see if the discussion compression alone is enough before hunting for more cuts.

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for trained reasoning.}\nThese findings illuminate why trained reasoning internalization (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves $86\\%$ on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\\sim}62\\%$; Bound~1). Rather than extracting symbolic structure that inherits generator biases, these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the bottleneck entirely.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). 63	
64	\paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}
65	We introduce \textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify these diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:
66	\begin{equation}
67	\label{eq:cil}
68	\text{CIL}(V, G) = \log \frac{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ wrong})}{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ correct})}.
69	\end{equation}
70	CIL measures whether verifier $V$ provides complementary information beyond generator $G$: $\text{CIL} > 0$ indicates $V$ preferentially corrects $G$'s errors (rescue), $\text{CIL} = 0$ indicates independence, and $\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias ($V$ fails when $G$ fails). CIL subsumes our key findings: (1)~Symbolic Confirmation Bias corresponds to $\text{CIL}(\text{self}, G) \approx 0$, since self-extracted constraints inherit the generator's bias; (2)~Observation~2 predicts $\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is architecturally independent and sufficiently capable; (3)~DRC adaptively routes to the verifier on questions where expected CIL is highest (low SC agreement signals high $G$-uncertainty, maximizing $P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ wrong})$). Across [N] generator--verifier pairs, CIL correlates with observed combo gain (Spearman $\rho = $ [TBD], $p < $ [TBD]), confirming that information-theoretic complementarity, not verifier accuracy alone, determines verification effectiveness.
71

CIL subsumes our findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\approx 0$; (2)~Observation~2 predicts $\\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is independent and capable; (3)~DRC routes to $V$ where expected CIL is highest. Across [N] pairs, CIL correlates with combo gain ($\\rho = $ [TBD], $p < $ [TBD]).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\\rho$) in \\citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Bound 1 (Self-Extraction Ceiling).}\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self} \\lesssim \\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Observation 2 (Capability Gap Condition).}\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Bound~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}\nWe introduce \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify these diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\nCIL measures whether verifier $V$ provides complementary information beyond generator $G$: $\\text{CIL} > 0$ indicates $V$ preferentially corrects $G$'s errors (rescue), $\\text{CIL} = 0$ indicates independence, and $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias ($V$ fails when $G$ fails). CIL subsumes our key findings: (1)~Symbolic Confirmation Bias corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\approx 0$, since self-extracted constraints inherit the generator's bias; (2)~Observation~2 predicts $\\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is architecturally independent and sufficiently capable; (3)~DRC adaptively routes to the verifier on questions where expected CIL is highest (low SC agreement signals high $G$-uncertainty, maximizing $P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})$). Across [N] generator--verifier pairs, CIL correlates with observed combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = $ [TBD], $p < $ [TBD]), confirming that information-theoretic complementarity, not verifier accuracy alone, determines verification effectiveness.\n",

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026 ARR: 8-page main text limit, Limitations/References/Appendix exempt
   - Symbolic Confirmation Bias (SCB): BR=4.97× Mistral, 3.93× Qwen, 2.88× LLaMA
   - CIL (Cumulative Information Lift): log(P(V correct|G wrong)/P(V correct|G correct))
   - Three paradigms: specification-generation, program-generation, trace-extraction

   - BR CIs: Mistral [3.98, 6.37], Qwen [2.44, 7.11], LLaMA raw CI removed (wrong definition), LLaMA weighted CI [2.19, 3.28]
   - DRC: τ=0.7 recovers 90% of gain at 39% cost (τ selected on eval set, not held-out)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **introduction.tex** (lines 24-26):
     - C4 §1 novelty argument: community assumption + strongest test
     ```latex
     Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction; neurosymbolic methods implicitly assume that formalizing into logic provides a reliable verification channel.
     We test the strongest version of this assumption: first-order logic checked by a provably sound SMT solver (Z3), which \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints.
     That this stronger channel still fails is the central surprise: Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).
     ```

   - **related_work.tex**:
     - C5 three paradigms + C4 SatLM control in neurosymbolic paragraph (line 8)
     - W7 ToT citation added to test-time compute paragraph (line 11): `\citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \citep{yao2023tree}`
     - W6 SCB distinction in CJT paragraph (line 15): "Symbolic confirmation bias is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}"
     - miya2025eidoku added to recent extensions list

   - **solutions.tex**:
     - W1 DRC τ caveat (line 66 area): "Note that $\tau$ was selected on the evaluation set; deployment would require a held-out validation split."
     - W2 Cross-arch 3 conditions in "Why it works" paragraph: "(i) structural independence, (ii) positive capability gap, (iii) task-verifier alignment"

   - **experiments.tex**:
     - W4 TOST margin: "reflecting the smallest $\Delta$ we consider practically meaningful given SC baselines of 39--86%"
     - TOST correction: "4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$"
     - Table 1 footnote simplified

   - **analysis.tex**:

     - Figure 2 → figure* full-width
     - BR with CIs (line 47):
     ```latex
     Bias generalizes: $4.97\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-bias}).
     ```

   - **method.tex** — CIL framework added after Observation 2 (after line 62):
     ```latex
     \paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}
     We introduce \textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify these diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:
     \begin{equation}
     \label{eq:cil}
     \text{CIL}(V, G) = \log \frac{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ wrong})}{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ correct})}.
     \end{equation}
     $\text{CIL} > 0$ means $V$ preferentially corrects $Gs errors; $\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. CIL subsumes our findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\text{CIL}(\text{self}, G) \approx 0$; (2)~Observation~2 predicts $\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is independent and capable; (3)~DRC routes to $V$ where expected CIL is highest. Across [N] pairs, CIL correlates with combo gain ($\rho = $ [TBD], $p < $ [TBD]).
     ```

   - **discussion.tex** — Compressed from 4 paragraphs to 3 (merged "Implications for trained reasoning" into "Test-time scaling"):
     ```latex
     \paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
     Our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.

     \paragraph{Test-time scaling.}
     $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$, confirming a structural ceiling \citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Trained reasoning internalization bypasses the bottleneck: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves 86\% on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\sim}62\%$; Bound~1).

     \paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
     Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
     ```

        "content": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\\rho$) in \\citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Bound 1 (Self-Extraction Ceiling).}\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self} \\lesssim \\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Observation 2 (Capability Gap Condition).}\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Bound~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}\nWe introduce \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify these diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ means $V$ preferentially corrects $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. CIL subsumes our findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\approx 0$; (2)~Observation~2 predicts $\\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is independent and capable; (3)~DRC routes to $V$ where expected CIL is highest. Across [N] pairs, CIL correlates with combo gain ($\\rho = $ [TBD], $p < $ [TBD]).\n",
        "numLines": 71,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 71
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/method.tex"
  },

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nOur bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$, confirming a structural ceiling \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Trained reasoning internalization bypasses the bottleneck: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves 86\\% on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\\sim}62\\%$; Bound~1).\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 12
      }

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. 1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	Our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$, confirming a structural ceiling \citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Trained reasoning internalization bypasses the bottleneck: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves 86\% on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\sim}62\%$; Bound~1).
9	
10	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
11	Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
12	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VRwZg7eMFXu1KrwBMNSCuP`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i) \emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii) \emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC (Observation~2); (iii) \emph{task-verifier alignment}---the NLI classification format naturally matches the premise-conclusion structure of logical reasoning tasks. Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	We evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator. Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces effective degrees of freedom, making the permutation test conservative.
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
20	\vspace{-3mm}
21	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases strongly monotonically (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}
22	\label{fig:capability-gap}
23	\vspace{-6mm}
24	\end{figure}
25	
26	\begin{table}[t]
27	\centering
28	\small
29	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
30	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
31	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
32	\toprule
33	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
34	\midrule
35	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
36	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
37	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
38	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
39	\bottomrule
40	\end{tabular}}
41	\end{table}
42	
43	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
44	\label{sec:drc}
45	
46	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
47	
48	\begin{table}[t]
49	\centering
50	\small
51	\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\% of questions.}
52	\label{tab:drc}
53	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
54	\toprule
55	Method & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\
56	\midrule
57	SC only ($\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% \\
58	DRC $\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\% & 65/600 & 10.8\% \\
59	\textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} \\
60	DRC $\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\% & 455/600 & 75.8\% \\
61	Full combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% \\
62	\bottomrule
63	\end{tabular}
64	\end{table}
65	
66	DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\,pp out of $+6.67$\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Observation~2). Note that $\tau$ was selected on the evaluation set; deployment would require a held-out validation split.
67	
68	
```

      "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i) \\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii) \\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC (Observation~2); (iii) \\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the NLI classification format naturally matches the premise-conclusion structure of logical reasoning tasks. Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator. Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces effective degrees of freedom, making the permutation test conservative.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases strongly monotonically (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Observation~2). Note that $\\tau$ was selected on the evaluation set; deployment would require a held-out validation split.\n\n",
      "numLines": 68,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 68
    }
  },

Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps.   "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch] R11 审稿修复 — Batch 1（纯文本修改，不依赖新数据）\n\nR11 Fresh Review 3.0 (Findings)，以下是经 Reviewer 审查后定稿的修复清单。DDL May 25，请尽快完成。\n\n---\n\n**1. N1 — ρ=1.0 降级 + 减少重复**\n\nρ=1.0 当前在论文中出现 11 次。减少到 ≤3 次：\n- **保留**：solutions.tex primary result 段（1次）、appendix 详细表格（1次）、capability-gap figure caption（1次）\n- **其余 8 处**：移除 ρ=1.0 或改为描述性语言 \"a consistent monotonic trend\" / \"monotonically ordered\"\n\n所有保留的 ρ=1.0 处添加 caveat：\n\"(exact permutation test, p=0.003; however, four of six conditions share the DeBERTa verifier, which may reduce effective degrees of freedom — see Limitations)\"\n\n在 Limitations section 添加一段：\n\"The capability-gap analysis rests on n=6 verifier–generator conditions, of which four share the same DeBERTa verifier. While each condition pairs the verifier with a different generator (producing gains ranging from +0.5pp to +6.67pp), the shared verifier component may inflate apparent consistency. A verifier-collapsed analysis with only three independent verifiers (DeBERTa-pooled, RoBERTa, BART) retains the monotonic ordering but at p=0.167, below conventional significance. We therefore characterize the capability-gap relationship as a consistent empirical pattern rather than a statistically confirmed law.\"\n\n（p=0.167 数字是 1/3! = 1/6 的精确值，可以直接写）\n\n**2. N2 — 标题/Abstract 限定规模**\n\n标题改为（暂用 7B-32B，70B 结果到手后可能更新）：\n\"Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Symbolic Constraints Do Not Improve LLM Reasoning at the 7B–32B Scale\"\n\nAbstract 修改：\n- \"do not improve majority voting at any capability level\" → \"we find no evidence of improvement across six open-source models (7B–32B), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two (both in the lowest-accuracy regime), and ten conditions remaining underpowered\"\n- 同步更新 Introduction 中类似的 unconditional negative claim\n\n**3. N3 — TOST margin justification**\n\n在 experiments.tex TOST 段添加 margin justification：\n\"We set the equivalence margin at ±2 percentage points, corresponding to the lower bound of gains typically reported in the self-consistency literature (Wang et al., 2023, report improvements of 2–5pp across benchmarks). A margin below 2pp would be too stringent to detect practically meaningful improvements, while ±3pp approaches the upper range of reported SC gains. Sensitivity analysis confirms robustness: at ±1pp, zero conditions achieve equivalence (0/2/14); at ±3pp, ten achieve equivalence (10/0/6). Notably, the asymmetry of an absolute margin means ±2pp is more conservative for high-accuracy conditions (±2.4% relative at 85% baseline) than low-accuracy ones (±5.1% relative at 39%), working against our equivalence claim in the regime where SICA most plausibly helps.\"\n\n将 sensitivity table 从附录移至正文（或在正文添加 inline summary）。\n\n**4. W1 — 伪形式化降级**\n\n- method.tex: \"Proposition 1\" → \"Empirical Finding 1\"；移除 theorem 环境，改为 shaded remark box\n- method.tex: \"Corollary 1\" → \"Empirical Finding 2\"（同样降级）\n- method.tex: \"≲\" → \"is empirically bounded by\"\n- method.tex:64 \"Proof sketch\" → \"Empirical support\"\n- 全文搜索 \"Proposition\" 和 \"Corollary\" 确保所有引用同步更新\n\n**5. W3 — Single-seed 限制更显著**\n\nLimitations section 扩展：\n\"Most individual conditions use a single random seed. Four-seed replication on the primary Mistral-7B FOLIO condition shows a Δ range of approximately 4 percentage points (+3.43 to −0.49), which spans the TOST equivalence margin. We therefore base our main conclusions on aggregate statistics across multiple conditions rather than individual point estimates.\"\n\n**6. W4 — Introduction formal channel distinction 前移**\n\n在 Introduction 引用 Huang et al. / Stechly et al. 后（大约第 2-3 段），添加：\n\"Prior work has shown that LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction (Huang et al., 2024) and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable (Stechly et al., 2024). Symbolic consensus introduces an additional assumption: that translating reasoning into first-order logic and aggregating via a provably sound solver (Z3) provides \\emph{independent} verification evidence. Unlike natural-language self-correction, where both generation and evaluation use the same neural channel, our formal channel is provably sound—the failure is therefore precisely localizable to the extraction step, not the verification mechanism. We show this assumption fails not because the solver is unreliable (99\\% of extracted constraints are syntactically valid), but because the extraction process inherits the model's own biases, creating \\emph{formalized echo chambers}.\"\n\n从 Related Work 末段移除重复内容（避免说两遍）。\n\n**7. W5 — Contribution 2 量化定位**\n\nContribution 2 描述修改为包含：\n\"We do not claim to discover a new diversity–accuracy tradeoff, which is well established in ensemble learning (Krogh \\& Vedelsby, 1994; Brown et al., 2005). Our contribution is quantifying its specific form in LLM self-extraction via three operationalizable diagnostics: effective sample size K_eff, symbolic confirmation bias ratio BR, and multi-candidate rate C_0.\"\n\n**8. W6 — BR tautological 承认**\n\nDiscussion section 添加：\n\"We acknowledge that BR > 1 is, in a sense, an expected consequence of self-extraction: a model that produces answer A will naturally generate constraints supporting A. The finding is not the direction of the bias but its magnitude—BR = 4.97× for Mistral means erroneous constraints outnumber correct ones by nearly 5:1 on problems the model answers incorrectly. This magnitude is consistent across architectures (4.97×, 3.93×, 2.88× for three model families), ruling out single-model artifacts. Practically, BR serves as a deployment-time failure predictor: high BR signals that symbolic aggregation will amplify rather than correct errors.\"\n\n注意：不要使用 \"per-condition variation (1.2×–12.3×)\" 数据——这不在当前论文中。\n\n**9. [Director 指令] [Patch] R11 审稿修复 — Batch 1（纯文本修改，不依赖新数据）

R11 Fresh Review 3.0 (Findings)，以下是经 Reviewer 审查后定稿的修复清单。DDL May 25，请尽快完成。

---

**1. N1 — ρ=1.0 降级 + 减少重复**

ρ=1.0 当前在论文中出现 11 次。减少到 ≤3 次：
- **保留**：solutions.tex primary result 段（1次）、appendix 详细表格（1次）、capability-gap figure caption（1次）
- **其余 8 处**：移除 ρ=1.0 或改为描述性语言 "a consistent monotonic trend" / "monotonically ordered"

所有保留的 ρ=1.0 处添加 caveat：
"(exact permutation test, p=0.003; however, four of six conditions share the DeBERTa verifier, which may reduce effective degrees of freedom — see Limitations)"

在 Limitations section 添加一段：
"The capability-gap analysis rests on n=6 verifier–generator conditions, of which four share the same DeBERTa verifier. While each condition pairs the verifier with a different generator (producing gains ranging from +0.5pp to +6.67pp), the shared verifier component may inflate apparent consistency. A verifier-collapsed analysis with only three independent verifiers (DeBERTa-pooled, RoBERTa, BART) retains the monotonic ordering but at p=0.167, below conventional significance. We therefore characterize the capability-gap relationship as a consistent empirical pattern rather than a statistically confirmed law."

（p=0.167 数字是 1/3! = 1/6 的精确值，可以直接写）

**2. N2 — 标题/Abstract 限定规模**

标题改为（暂用 7B-32B，70B 结果到手后可能更新）：
"Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Symbolic Constraints Do Not Improve LLM Reasoning at the 7B–32B Scale"

Abstract 修改：
- "do not improve majority voting at any capability level" → "we find no evidence of improvement across six open-source models (7B–32B), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two (both in the lowest-accuracy regime), and ten conditions remaining underpowered"
- 同步更新 Introduction 中类似的 unconditional negative claim

**3. N3 — TOST margin justification**

在 experiments.tex TOST 段添加 margin justification：
"We set the equivalence margin at ±2 percentage points, corresponding to the lower bound of gains typically reported in the self-consistency literature (Wang et al., 2023, report improvements of 2–5pp across benchmarks). A margin below 2pp would be too stringent to detect practically meaningful improvements, while ±3pp approaches the upper range of reported SC gains. Sensitivity analysis confirms robustness: at ±1pp, zero conditions achieve equivalence (0/2/14); at ±3pp, ten achieve equivalence (10/0/6). Notably, the asymmetry of an absolute margin means ±2pp is more conservative for high-accuracy conditions (±2.4% relative at 85% baseline) than low-accuracy ones (±5.1% relative at 39%), working against our equivalence claim in the regime where SICA most plausibly helps."

将 sensitivity table 从附录移至正文（或在正文添加 inline summary）。

**4. W1 — 伪形式化降级**

- method.tex: "Proposition 1" → "Empirical Finding 1"；移除 theorem 环境，改为 shaded remark box
- method.tex: "Corollary 1" → "Empirical Finding 2"（同样降级）
- method.tex: "≲" → "is empirically bounded by"
- method.tex:64 "Proof sketch" → "Empirical support"
- 全文搜索 "Proposition" 和 "Corollary" 确保所有引用同步更新

**5. W3 — Single-seed 限制更显著**

Limitations section 扩展：
"Most individual conditions use a single random seed. Four-seed replication on the primary Mistral-7B FOLIO condition shows a Δ range of approximately 4 percentage points (+3.43 to −0.49), which spans the TOST equivalence margin. We therefore base our main conclusions on aggregate statistics across multiple conditions rather than individual point estimates."

**6. W4 — Introduction formal channel distinction 前移**

在 Introduction 引用 Huang et al. / Stechly et al. 后（大约第 2-3 段），添加：
"Prior work has shown that LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction (Huang et al., 2024) and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable (Stechly et al., 2024). Symbolic consensus introduces an additional assumption: that translating reasoning into first-order logic and aggregating via a provably sound solver (Z3) provides \emph{independent} verification evidence. Unlike natural-language self-correction, where both generation and evaluation use the same neural channel, our formal channel is provably sound—the failure is therefore precisely localizable to the extraction step, not the verification mechanism. We show this assumption fails not because the solver is unreliable (99\% of extracted constraints are syntactically valid), but because the extraction process inherits the model's own biases, creating \emph{formalized echo chambers}."

从 Related Work 末段移除重复内容（避免说两遍）。

**7. W5 — Contribution 2 量化定位**

Contribution 2 描述修改为包含：
"We do not claim to discover a new diversity–accuracy tradeoff, which is well established in ensemble learning (Krogh \& Vedelsby, 1994; Brown et al., 2005). Our contribution is quantifying its specific form in LLM self-extraction via three operationalizable diagnostics: effective sample size K_eff, symbolic confirmation bias ratio BR, and multi-candidate rate C_0."

**8. W6 — BR tautological 承认**

Discussion section 添加：
"We acknowledge that BR > 1 is, in a sense, an expected consequence of self-extraction: a model that produces answer A will naturally generate constraints supporting A. The finding is not the direction of the bias but its magnitude—BR = 4.97× for Mistral means erroneous constraints outnumber correct ones by nearly 5:1 on problems the model answers incorrectly. This magnitude is consistent across architectures (4.97×, 3.93×, 2.88× for three model families), ruling out single-model artifacts. Practically, BR serves as a deployment-time failure predictor: high BR signals that symbolic aggregation will amplify rather than correct errors."

注意：不要使用 "per-condition variation (1.2×–12.3×)" 数据——这不在当前论文中。

**9. W7 — 术语品牌化降级**

- "Independence Bottleneck" 当前出现约 8 次 → 减至 2-3 次（首次定义处 + section heading + conclusion），其余替换为 "the independence limitation" / "correlated same-model voting"
- "Symbolic Mirage" 保留 section heading + 定义处 + 1-2 次引用，其余偶尔用 "the confirmation bias mechanism"
- "Symbolic Confirmation Bias" 保留不变（核心术语）

**10. W8 — 遗漏引用**

需要添加的引用（先验证 arXiv 可用性再加）：
1. **Byerly & Khashabi (2026)** "Self-Consistency Falls Short!" — cite in §2 Related Work, SC limitations 段
2. **arxiv:2604.07650 (2026)** "How Independent are Large Language Models?" — cite in §2, 在 ensemble diversity 段落深入讨论（不只 cite），区分他们的 behavioral-level BEI 和我们的 process-level K_eff
3. **arxiv:2506.07962 (2025)** "Correlated Errors in Large Language Models" — cite in §2
4. **arxiv:2510.01499 (2025)** "Beyond Majority Voting" — cite in §2, aggregation methods 段
5. **arxiv:2501.19306 (2025)** "SETS: Self-Verification and Self-Correction" — cite in §7 Discussion

对 2604.07650 的讨论建议：
"Concurrent work by [authors] proposes Behavioral Entanglement Index (BEI) and Cumulative Information Gain (CIG) to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies in LLM ensembles. Our analysis is complementary: while BEI operates at the behavioral (answer) level across different models, our Process-κ and K_eff diagnostics operate at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces, capturing a finer-grained form of entanglement that behavioral metrics would miss. Furthermore, we identify a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that their framework does not address."

**11. W9 — 27 策略 + trained extractor**

Section 5.5 或 Discussion 区分策略类型：
"Of our 27 remediation attempts, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). The exhaustive search strengthens our negative conclusion but we note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., models fine-tuned on oracle constraint annotations). Our oracle controls show that the extraction-quality ceiling is +29.90pp, suggesting that even a moderately improved extractor could yield practical gains. We consider this the most promising direction for future work."

**12. VL1 — Capability-gap scatter 上提**

将 appendix 中的 capability-gap scatter plot 上提到 §6 主文（cross-architecture results section）。如果空间紧张，可以缩小为 column-width figure。这比纯数字描述更直观。
